# SparseComm WWW experiments in notebooks

Start with **00_Setup_Data_Protocol.ipynb**, then **01_E0_Legacy_Provenance_Cost_Audit.ipynb**. Everything newly created for this request is under `WEBExtension`. Existing project files and results are read without modification.

| Notebook | Experiment / output |
|---|---|
| 00_Setup_Data_Protocol | Authentic dataset verification, frozen sample, retrieval availability, design diagram |
| 01_E0_Legacy_Provenance_Cost_Audit | Recreated legacy tables, per-question scores, source/artifact checksums, corrected/original distinctions |
| 02_E1_Evidence_Availability | Redundant versus partitioned evidence; NoComm, SelfUpdate, CW, CCW |
| 03_E2_Balanced_Recipient_Routing | Opposite cycles with identical source messages, balanced calls and exact payload matching |
| 04_E3_Exact_Budget_Content | Fixed receiver prefix/crop intervention with exactly matched tokenizer counts |
| 05_E4_Shared_Trajectory_Stopping | Full trajectories and unanimous-prefix replay, quality/call tradeoffs |
| 06_Statistics_Tables_Figures￼

You said MASTER PROMPT: Interactive Functional Analysis Tutor, Visualizer, and Exam Preparation System ROLE …
MASTER PROMPT: Interactive Functional Analysis Tutor, Visualizer, and Exam Preparation System



ROLE AND OBJECTIVE



Act as a world-class Functional Analysis professor, mathematical researcher, cognitive learning specialist, examination evaluator, and interactive educational visualization developer.



I will provide a collection of Functional Analysis practice questions, either as a PDF, image, document, or plain text.



Your task is NOT simply to solve these questions.



Your mission is to transform every question into a deeply intuitive, mathematically rigorous, visually interactive, memorable, and exam-oriented learning experience.



Assume I want to:



Understand every mathematical concept from first principles.



Develop intuition strong enough to reconstruct proofs independently.



Visualize abstract mathematical ideas using interactive diagrams, motion, and animations.



Learn exactly how to approach unfamiliar examination questions.



Write mathematically rigorous, well-structured answers that maximize examination marks.



Remember important definitions, theorems, proof techniques, and solutions over the long term.



Connect Functional Analysis to machine learning, reinforcement learning, optimization, neural networks, and AI research.



Become capable of solving variations of each question without memorizing the entire solution mechanically.



FINAL OUTPUT REQUIREMENT: Produce a complete, self-contained interactive HTML learning application, with embedded CSS and JavaScript, that I can open in a browser and use to study every question sequentially.



PHASE 1: ANALYZE THE ENTIRE QUESTION PAPER



Before teaching or solving anything, carefully read every uploaded question.



Extract and preserve the original wording, notation, assumptions, and numbering.



If any part of a question is unclear or illegible, explicitly flag it rather than inventing missing information.



Construct a dependency map identifying:



All mathematical topics involved.



Definitions required for each question.



Theorems and lemmas needed.



Prerequisite concepts.



Connections between different questions.



Repeated proof techniques.



Relative difficulty.



Expected examination answer length, when inferable.



Organize questions into a learning sequence that respects prerequisite dependencies, while preserving their original question numbers.



Create an interactive dashboard containing:



Total number of questions.



Topic-wise grouping.



Concept dependency graph.



Difficulty indicators.



Estimated study time.



Learning progress.



Questions mastered.



Questions requiring revision.



Do not begin with a large collection of unexplained formulas.



Build understanding systematically.



PHASE 2: TEACH THE PREREQUISITES BEFORE EVERY QUESTION



For each question, identify exactly which mathematical concepts I need.



Teach these concepts BEFORE revealing the complete solution.



Use the following teaching structure.



A. Motivation: Why does this concept exist?



Explain the mathematical problem that motivated the concept.



Start with a simple example and gradually develop the formal abstraction.



B. Intuitive explanation



Explain the concept using:



Geometric intuition.



Physical analogies where appropriate.



Simple numerical examples.



Everyday analogies that preserve mathematical accuracy.



Visual representations.



Explain what the concept actually means, not merely its formal definition.



C. Formal mathematical definition



Present the rigorous definition with all relevant quantifiers, assumptions, and notation.



Explain every mathematical symbol.



D. Examples and counterexamples



Provide at least:



One simple example.



One nontrivial example.



One counterexample showing why an assumption matters, when applicable.



E. Connection to previously learned concepts



Explain how this concept builds upon earlier material.



F. AI and machine learning connections



Whenever mathematically justified, relate the concept to:



Neural network optimization.



Gradient descent and convergence.



Reinforcement learning.



Bellman operators and contraction mappings.



Hilbert spaces and kernel methods.



Function approximation.



Operator theory.



Infinite-dimensional optimization.



Probability and expectations.



Regularization and stability.



Explain the mathematical connection precisely, not through superficial analogies.



Clearly distinguish finite-dimensional intuition from infinite-dimensional results.



G. Prerequisite checkpoint



Before moving forward, give me 2–3 short questions to verify understanding.



Provide optional hints and explanations.



PHASE 3: DECODE WHAT EACH EXAM QUESTION IS ACTUALLY ASKING



Before solving a question, display a dedicated Question Decoder.



Include:



Original question


Display the exact question.



2. Given information



List the hypotheses, mathematical objects, spaces, operators, and conditions.



3. What must be proved or calculated?



State the exact mathematical objective.



4. Hidden mathematical clues



Explain why particular words suggest particular theorems or techniques.



For example:



"Complete" may suggest Banach spaces or Cauchy sequence arguments.



"Bounded linear operator" suggests operator norms and continuity.



"Contraction" suggests the Banach fixed-point theorem.



"Orthogonal" suggests inner products, projections, or Hilbert space geometry.



"Weak convergence" suggests dual-space functionals.



These are possible clues, not automatic theorem choices.



5. First thought in the examination



Explain what I should think within the first 20–30 seconds of seeing the question.



6. Common traps



Identify typical misunderstandings and invalid assumptions.



7. Solution strategy



Give a concise roadmap before the proof.



PHASE 4: BUILD INTERACTIVE VISUAL INTUITION WITH MOTION



This is one of the MOST IMPORTANT requirements.



For every question, design an interactive visualization that helps me understand the underlying mathematical idea.



Use HTML5 Canvas, SVG, CSS animations, and vanilla JavaScript as appropriate.



Use genuine mathematical animations rather than decorative motion.



Where possible, include:



Play, pause, restart, and step-forward controls.



Animation speed controls.



Sliders to change mathematical parameters.



Hover explanations.



Interactive examples.



Side-by-side comparisons.



Highlighted proof steps.



Animated transformations.



A reset-to-default button.



Visualization examples



Normed spaces: Animate different unit balls under different norms, such as ℓ₁, ℓ₂, and ℓ∞.



Cauchy sequences: Show points approaching one another and illustrate the distinction between being Cauchy and converging inside a space.



Completeness: Visualize sequences whose limits belong or do not belong to the underlying space.



Linear operators: Animate vectors undergoing linear transformations, showing boundedness and norm changes.



Banach fixed-point theorem: Animate repeated application of a contraction, with iterates approaching a fixed point.



Hilbert spaces: Animate orthogonal projections, distances, and decompositions.



Weak versus strong convergence: Use carefully constructed examples to illustrate what each type of convergence requires.



Operator norms: Show how the maximum stretching factor relates to the norm of an operator in finite-dimensional examples.



Hahn–Banach theorem: Illustrate geometric separation or extension ideas in finite-dimensional settings, while explaining the limits of the visualization.



Mandatory mathematical accuracy



Whenever an infinite-dimensional concept is illustrated in two dimensions, explicitly label the visualization as a finite-dimensional analogy.



Do not imply that a diagram proves a general theorem.



If a meaningful animation is impossible, create an interactive proof diagram, dependency map, or example explorer instead.



For each visualization, include:



What am I seeing?



What mathematical object does each visual element represent?



What changes during the animation?



What mathematical property remains invariant?



How does this visualization help prove the result?



What should I remember from it?



PHASE 5: SOLVE EVERY QUESTION STEP BY STEP



Do not jump directly to the final proof.



Use progressive disclosure.



Layer 1: Intuitive solution



Explain the core idea in ordinary language.



Describe why the result should be true.



Layer 2: Mathematical strategy



Identify the theorem, definition, construction, or contradiction argument needed.



Explain why that strategy is appropriate.



Layer 3: Rigorous derivation



Provide the complete mathematical solution.



For every meaningful step:



Show the mathematical expression.



Explain why the step is valid.



Identify the theorem or property used.



Explain how it contributes to the final conclusion.



Do not skip important algebraic or logical steps.



Pay special attention to quantifiers, limiting arguments, domain assumptions, completeness, boundedness, and continuity.



Layer 4: Proof verification



Check:



Whether every assumption was used correctly.



Whether all theorem conditions hold.



Whether any circular reasoning occurred.



Whether the conclusion matches the question.



Whether the proof works in infinite-dimensional spaces when required.



Layer 5: Alternative method



Where useful, provide another valid proof or a shorter examination method.



Explain which method is easier to remember and which is more efficient under examination conditions.



PHASE 6: CONVERT THE SOLUTION INTO A FULL-MARKS EXAM ANSWER



After the detailed explanation, provide a separate section titled:



"What to Write in the Examination"



This must be a polished, mathematically rigorous answer suitable for directly reproducing in an examination.



Follow this structure:



State relevant definitions or theorem assumptions.



Establish the required mathematical setup.



Present the proof in logical order.



Justify important steps.



Conclude clearly with the required result.



Make the answer concise enough for a timed examination but complete enough to avoid losing marks through unjustified steps.



If the marks allocation is available, adjust the answer length accordingly.



If marks are not provided, explain the assumed answer depth.



Also provide:



Examiner's marking perspective



Which statements are essential?



Which mathematical steps are likely to earn marks?



Which omissions could lose marks?



Which statements are optional?



What are common mistakes?



Do not invent an official marking scheme. Clearly label any mark distribution as an estimate.



Provide two answer modes:



Full Proof Mode: Complete rigorous examination solution.



Quick Revision Mode: Shortest mathematically sufficient proof, when one exists.



PHASE 7: MAKE ME REMEMBER THE PROOF PERMANENTLY



I do not want to memorize a proof as meaningless symbols.



I want to remember the reasoning so strongly that I can reconstruct it under examination pressure.



For every question, build a Proof Memory System.



A. Proof skeleton



Compress the entire proof into 3–7 logical steps.



B. Memory anchors



Create memorable verbal or visual cues for the main ideas.



C. Story-based encoding



When appropriate, transform the proof into a meaningful story or spatial metaphor without compromising the mathematics.



D. Visual memory map



Create a diagram connecting:



Given assumptions → Key theorem → Critical argument → Conclusion.



E. Recall without looking



Hide the solution and ask me to reconstruct the proof.



Provide hints progressively:



Hint 1: Identify the theorem.



Hint 2: Recall the key construction.



Hint 3: Recall the crucial inequality or argument.



Hint 4: Reveal the next logical step.



F. Fill-in-the-proof exercise



Remove key statements from the proof and ask me to complete them.



G. Error detection exercise



Show a plausible but incorrect proof and ask me to identify the mistake.



Then explain the correction.



H. Spaced repetition



Create a revision schedule with checkpoints such as:



Immediately after learning.



After 1 day.



After 3 days.



After 7 days.



After 14 days.



Make the intervals adjustable.



I. Active recall flashcards



Generate flashcards for:



Definitions.



Theorem hypotheses.



Theorem conclusions.



Proof strategies.



Counterexamples.



Key inequalities.



Common mistakes.



J. Personal memory hook



Relate the central proof idea to an AI or machine learning concept I am likely to recognize.



For example, connect contraction mappings to convergence of Bellman iterations in reinforcement learning when the relevant assumptions hold.



Prioritize understanding and reconstruction over rote memorization.



PHASE 8: TRAIN ME TO SOLVE UNSEEN QUESTIONS



After completing each original question, generate:



An easier warm-up variation.



A similar examination-level question.



A more challenging variation.



A conceptual question testing the theorem's assumptions.



An AI-related application, when appropriate.



Let me attempt each question before revealing the solution.



Provide a hint ladder rather than immediately showing the answer.



Explain how changing the hypotheses changes the solution.



Teach me to recognize the underlying mathematical pattern, not merely the wording.



PHASE 9: CREATE A COMPLETE INTERACTIVE HTML LEARNING APPLICATION



Return the learning experience as a functional HTML application.



Technical requirements



Use:



HTML5.



Responsive CSS.



Vanilla JavaScript.



SVG and/or Canvas for interactive mathematical diagrams.



MathJax or KaTeX for mathematical notation, if external dependencies are allowed.



Prefer a single self-contained HTML file.



If an external mathematics-rendering library is required, clearly disclose that internet access is needed unless the library is bundled locally.



Do not claim the application works completely offline if it relies on CDN resources.



All buttons, sliders, animations, quizzes, navigation controls, and interactive components must be functional.



Do not create decorative buttons that do nothing.



Use localStorage where available to preserve progress, quiz results, bookmarks, and revision status.



Application layout



Dashboard



Show all questions, topic categories, learning progress, and revision status.



Concept Classroom



Interactive explanations of prerequisites.



Question Decoder



Given information, required result, clues, and strategy.



Visualization Lab



Mathematical animations with interactive controls.



Guided Solution



Step-by-step reasoning with progressive reveal.



Exam Answer



Clean, copyable, examination-ready mathematical proof.



Memory Palace



Proof skeletons, flashcards, memory anchors, and recall exercises.



Practice Arena



Related problems, hints, and solutions.



Revision Center



Spaced repetition, weak-topic tracking, and progress review.



Design requirements



Create an aesthetically polished interface with:



Clean typography.



Clear mathematical notation.



Light and dark themes.



Responsive layout for mobile, tablet, and desktop.



Sidebar or tab-based question navigation.



Smooth but purposeful transitions.



Progress indicators.



Keyboard-friendly controls.



Accessible contrast and labels.



Reduced-motion support.



Print-friendly examination answers.



Avoid unnecessary animations that distract from understanding.



Use color consistently to distinguish assumptions, mathematical transformations, intermediate steps, and conclusions.



PHASE 10: ADAPT TO MY LEARNING LEVEL



Act as an adaptive tutor.



Start with intuitive explanations, then gradually increase mathematical rigor.



When I answer incorrectly:



Identify the misconception.



Explain why my reasoning fails.



Give a simpler counterexample or visualization.



Ask a targeted follow-up question.



Revisit the original problem after the misconception is corrected.



When I answer correctly:



Confirm the reasoning.



Identify any missing justification.



Introduce a slightly harder variation.



Connect the concept to another topic.



Do not assume that viewing a solution means I have mastered it.



Track mastery separately from completion.



If automated assessment of free-form proofs is not reliably possible within a standalone HTML file, use self-assessment rubrics or structured checkpoints rather than pretending to grade arbitrary mathematical arguments.



PHASE 11: FINAL EXAMINATION REVISION



After all questions are completed, create an integrated revision section containing:



A concise Functional Analysis formula and theorem sheet.



A theorem dependency map.



Frequently used proof patterns.



Common counterexamples.



High-priority examination questions.



Short proof skeletons.



A timed mock examination based on the covered topics.



A self-assessment checklist.



A personalized revision queue based on quiz results.



Include a Last 30 Minutes Before the Exam mode.



This mode should show only the highest-value definitions, theorem hypotheses, proof triggers, critical inequalities, and memory anchors.



STRICT RULES



Never provide only final answers.



Never introduce a theorem without explaining its assumptions.



Never skip mathematically essential proof steps.



Never sacrifice correctness for visual simplicity.



Never confuse geometric intuition with formal proof.



Never use AI analogies that are mathematically misleading.



Never mark a question as mastered solely because I opened its solution.



Never fabricate missing question text or marking criteria.



Never create nonfunctional interactive elements.



Never omit a question from the uploaded practice set.



Never replace mathematical notation with vague verbal descriptions.



Never reveal the complete proof before providing an opportunity for guided reasoning, unless I explicitly choose to reveal it.



Ensure that the final examination answers are rigorous, concise, and easy to reproduce.



Prefer meaningful learning interactions over excessive visual effects.



If the question set is too large for a single reliable output, implement it in complete, manageable batches while maintaining a consistent application structure and preserving all question numbers.



EXECUTION INSTRUCTIONS



When I upload my practice questions:



Step 1: Read and extract every question.



Step 2: Analyze the prerequisites and construct the topic dependency map.



Step 3: Design the complete learning roadmap.



Step 4: Build the interactive HTML application and dashboard.



Step 5: Implement the first question as a fully functional learning module, including its concepts, mathematical visualizations, rigorous solution, exam answer, memory system, and practice exercises.



Step 6: Continue implementing the remaining questions systematically, preserving the same quality and interaction model.



Step 7: Verify mathematical correctness, rendering, navigation, animation controls, and quiz behavior.



Step 8: Deliver the completed HTML file or complete HTML source code, depending on the available environment.



Do not stop after producing a plan when implementation is possible.



ULTIMATE LEARNING GOAL



By the end of this experience, I should be able to look at any covered Functional Analysis question and:



Recognize the relevant mathematical structure.



Identify the appropriate theorem or proof technique.



Visualize the underlying mathematical idea.



Construct the proof independently.



Write a complete, rigorous examination answer.



Explain why the proof works.



Remember its logical structure.



Apply the same reasoning to unfamiliar questions.



Connect the result to relevant AI and machine learning problems.



Teach me Functional Analysis so that I understand it, can visualize it, can prove it, can remember it, and can reproduce it confidently in an examination.

PHASE 5A: DISCOVER THE EASIEST AND SMARTEST SOLUTION



For EVERY Functional Analysis question, actively investigate whether there is an easier, shorter, more intuitive, or more examination-friendly way to solve it.



Do not automatically assume that the textbook solution is the best solution.



Explore multiple solution methods


Whenever mathematically possible, provide:



Method A — Easiest Intuitive Method



Start with the simplest possible reasoning.
Minimize abstract machinery and unnecessary calculations.
Use definitions directly when that makes the proof easier.
Explain why the method works using visual intuition.


Method B — Shortest Exam Method



Find the shortest mathematically rigorous solution.
Prefer direct applications of known theorems when their hypotheses are satisfied.
Minimize writing time without sacrificing essential justifications.
Present the exact proof I should reproduce in the examination.


Method C — Standard Textbook Method



Explain the conventional proof typically taught in Functional Analysis.
Show the important mathematical steps and assumptions.


Method D — Clever Alternative Method



Explore alternative approaches such as contradiction, contrapositive, equivalent characterizations, sequence arguments, norm inequalities, or operator-theoretic methods.
Use these approaches only when they genuinely apply.
Highlight any elegant shortcut or useful mathematical insight.


Do not invent alternative methods merely to fill these categories. If only one practical method exists, say so.



2. Compare the methods



For every question with multiple valid approaches, provide a comparison table:



Method| Difficulty| Proof length| Time in exam| Easy to remember?

Intuitive| Easy/Medium/Hard| Short/Medium/Long| Estimated| Yes/No

Shortest exam| Easy/Medium/Hard| Short/Medium/Long| Estimated| Yes/No

Textbook| Easy/Medium/Hard| Short/Medium/Long| Estimated| Yes/No

Alternative| Easy/Medium/Hard| Short/Medium/Long| Estimated| Yes/No



3. Recommend the best method



After comparing the approaches, explicitly identify:



Easiest to understand
Shortest to write in the examination
Easiest to memorize
Most generally applicable
Best overall method for my examination


Explain why the recommended method is preferable.



4. Teach shortcut recognition



Explain how I can recognize the shortcut when encountering a similar question.



Include:



What clue in the question suggests the easier method?
Which definition or theorem makes the proof short?
Which unnecessary steps can be avoided?
Under what assumptions does the shortcut work?
When would the shortcut fail?


5. Create an animated comparison



In the HTML application, include an interactive Compare Solutions feature.



Allow me to switch between methods and see:



Animated proof flowcharts.
Step-by-step mathematical derivations.
Key differences highlighted.
Theorems used in each method.
Estimated examination writing time.
A side-by-side comparison of proof lengths.


Include a Show Easiest Solution button and a Show Shortest Exam Proof button.



Both buttons must reveal complete, mathematically correct solutions.



6. Memory optimization



For the recommended examination method, provide:



One-line proof idea: The central argument in a single sentence.



Three-step memory skeleton: Compress the proof into three memorable logical steps when possible.



Trigger words: Keywords that help me recall the method.



Reconstruction exercise: Hide the proof and guide me through reproducing it independently.



GOLDEN RULE



Never make a Functional Analysis problem unnecessarily complicated.



First search for the simplest rigorous solution. Then explain the deeper mathematical reasoning.



A shorter proof is preferable only when it is correct, complete, and appropriate for the examination.



My ultimate goal is not to memorize four proofs for every question. It is to understand the available methods, select the easiest reliable one, and remember it confidently.



Now analyze my uploaded practice questions and begin building the interactive HTML learning experience.

What's Inside the Interactive Application
Overview & Dependency Dashboard (Phase 1): Clickable SVG concept-dependency graph linking foundational theorems to Q1–Q6, live mastery counters, and a toggle between Original and Prerequisite learning sequences.

Concept Classroom (Phase 2 & Phase 10): First-principles motivation, geometric analogies, symbol-by-symbol definitions, examples/counterexamples, rigorous AI/ML operator-theoretic connections (Deep Equilibrium Models, Tikhonov regularization in RKHS, Kernel PCA, Low-Rank Adaptation), and adaptive checkpoints that diagnose specific misconceptions.

Question Decoder (Phase 3): Verbatim question and hint display, hypothesis-to-objective breakdown, hidden clue trigger table, first-30-seconds exam thought process, and common traps.

Interactive HTML5 Canvas Visualization Lab (Phase 4): Custom mathematical animations for every question with Play/Pause, Step Forward, Restart, speed controls, live parameter sliders (e.g., bound

Guided Solution & Compare Solutions Studio (Phase 5 & Phase 5A): Progressive step-by-step derivation reveal alongside an interactive comparison of Method A (Easiest Intuitive), Method B (Shortest Exam Proof), Method C (Standard Textbook), and Method D (Clever Alternative) with animated flowcharts and shortcut recognition guides.

Exam Answer & Memory Palace (Phases 6 & 7): Copyable and printable Full Proof Mode and Quick Revision Mode scripts, estimated examiner marking perspectives, 3–5 step proof skeletons, active reconstruction scratchpads with progressive hints, fill-in-the-proof dropdowns, "Spot the Fatal Flaw" error-detection exercises, flip flashcards, and adjustable spaced-repetition scheduling persisted in localStorage.

Practice Arena & Revision Center (Phases 8 & 11): Thirty graded unseen problems (5 per question) with hint ladders, a master theorem/counterexample reference sheet, a timed mock exam with live rubric scoring, and a Last 30 Minutes Before the Exam rapid-fire view.

Open the HTML file in any browser to start with Question 1 or switch to the Prerequisite Sequence from the top bar.

<style>
:root {
  --bg-primary: #f8fafc;
  --bg-surface: #ffffff;
  --bg-elevated: #f1f5f9;
  --bg-subtle: #e2e8f0;
  --text-primary: #0f172a;
  --text-secondary: #334155;
  --text-muted: #64748b;
  --border-color: #cbd5e1;
  --accent-primary: #1d4ed8;
  --accent-primary-light: #dbeafe;
  --accent-secondary: #0f766e;
  --accent-secondary-light: #ccfbf1;
  --color-assumption: #2563eb;
  --color-assumption-bg: #eff6ff;
  --color-transform: #d97706;
  --color-transform-bg: #fffbeb;
  --color-step: #7c3aed;
  --color-step-bg: #f5f3ff;
  --color-conclusion: #059669;
  --color-conclusion-bg: #ecfdf5;
  --color-danger: #dc2626;
  --color-danger-bg: #fef2f2;
  --shadow-sm: 0 1px 3px rgba(15, 23, 42, 0.08);
  --shadow-md: 0 4px 12px rgba(15, 23, 42, 0.08);
  --radius-sm: 6px;
  --radius-md: 10px;
  --radius-lg: 14px;
  --font-sans: 'Inter', -apple-system, BlinkMacSystemFont, 'Segoe UI', Roboto, sans-serif;
  --font-serif: 'STIX Two Text', 'Cambria Math', 'Georgia', serif;
  --font-mono: 'JetBrains Mono', 'Fira Code', 'Consolas', monospace;
  --transition-speed: 0.2s;
}

[data-theme="dark"] {
  --bg-primary: #0b1120;
  --bg-surface: #111827;
  --bg-elevated: #1e293b;
  --bg-subtle: #334155;
  --text-primary: #f8fafc;
  --text-secondary: #cbd5e1;
  --text-muted: #94a3b8;
  --border-color: #334155;
  --accent-primary: #60a5fa;
  --accent-primary-light: #1e3a8a;
  --accent-secondary: #2dd4bf;
  --accent-secondary-light: #134e4a;
  --color-assumption: #60a5fa;
  --color-assumption-bg: rgba(37, 99, 235, 0.15);
  --color-transform: #fbbf24;
  --color-transform-bg: rgba(217, 119, 6, 0.15);
  --color-step: #a78bfa;
  --color-step-bg: rgba(124, 58, 237, 0.15);
  --color-conclusion: #34d399;
  --color-conclusion-bg: rgba(5, 150, 105, 0.15);
  --color-danger: #f87171;
  --color-danger-bg: rgba(220, 38, 38, 0.15);
  --shadow-sm: 0 1px 3px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.4);
  --shadow-md: 0 4px 12px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.5);
}

[data-reduced-motion="true"] * {
  animation-duration: 0.001ms !important;
  transition-duration: 0.001ms !important;
}

* {
  box-sizing: border-box;
  margin: 0;
  padding: 0;
}

body {
  font-family: var(--font-sans);
  background-color: var(--bg-primary);
  color: var(--text-primary);
  line-height: 1.65;
  font-size: 15px;
  transition: background-color var(--transition-speed), color var(--transition-speed);
}

/* Header & Top Navigation */
.top-header {
  background: var(--bg-surface);
  border-bottom: 1px solid var(--border-color);
  position: sticky;
  top: 0;
  z-index: 100;
  box-shadow: var(--shadow-sm);
}

.header-inner {
  max-width: 1440px;
  margin: 0 auto;
  padding: 0.75rem 1.25rem;
  display: flex;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  justify-content: space-between;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 0.75rem;
}

.brand-block {
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
}

.brand-course {
  font-size: 1.05rem;
  font-weight: 800;
  color: var(--accent-primary);
  letter-spacing: -0.01em;
}

.brand-sub {
  font-size: 0.78rem;
  color: var(--text-muted);
}

.global-nav {
  display: flex;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  gap: 0.4rem;
  align-items: center;
}

.nav-btn {
  background: var(--bg-elevated);
  color: var(--text-secondary);
  border: 1px solid var(--border-color);
  padding: 0.45rem 0.85rem;
  border-radius: var(--radius-sm);
  font-size: 0.84rem;
  font-weight: 600;
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: all var(--transition-speed);
}

.nav-btn:hover {
  background: var(--accent-primary-light);
  color: var(--accent-primary);
}

.nav-btn.active {
  background: var(--accent-primary);
  color: #ffffff;
  border-color: var(--accent-primary);
}

.nav-btn.urgent-btn {
  background: var(--color-transform-bg);
  color: var(--color-transform);
  border-color: var(--color-transform);
}

.nav-btn.urgent-btn.active {
  background: var(--color-transform);
  color: #ffffff;
}

.utility-controls {
  display: flex;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  gap: 0.4rem;
  align-items: center;
}

.util-btn, .util-select {
  background: var(--bg-surface);
  color: var(--text-secondary);
  border: 1px solid var(--border-color);
  padding: 0.35rem 0.65rem;
  border-radius: var(--radius-sm);
  font-size: 0.78rem;

}

.util-btn:hover {
  border-color: var(--accent-primary);
  color: var(--accent-primary);
}

/* Color Semantics Legend Bar */
.semantics-bar {
  background: var(--bg-elevated);
  border-bottom: 1px solid var(--border-color);
  font-size: 0.76rem;
  padding: 0.35rem 1.25rem;
}

.semantics-inner {
  max-width: 1440px;
  margin: 0 auto;
  display: flex;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  justify-content: space-between;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 0.5rem;
}

.legend-items {
  display: flex;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  gap: 0.9rem;
  align-items: center;
}

.legend-dot {
  display: inline-block;
  width: 9px;
  height: 9px;
  border-radius: 50%;
  margin-right: 4px;
}

/* Main Container */
.main-container {
  max-width: 1440px;
  margin: 0 auto;
  padding: 1.25rem;
}

/* Cards & Grid Layouts */
.card {
  background: var(--bg-surface);
  border: 1px solid var(--border-color);
  border-radius: var(--radius-md);
  padding: 1.25rem;
  margin-bottom: 1.15rem;
  box-shadow: var(--shadow-sm);
}

.card-title {
  font-size: 1.08rem;
  font-weight: 700;
  margin-bottom: 0.75rem;
  color: var(--text-primary);
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  justify-content: space-between;
  gap: 0.5rem;
}

.kpi-grid {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(190px, 1fr));
  gap: 0.9rem;
  margin-bottom: 1.25rem;
}

.kpi-card {
  background: var(--bg-surface);
  border: 1px solid var(--border-color);
  border-left: 4px solid var(--accent-primary);
  border-radius: var(--radius-md);
  padding: 1rem;
  box-shadow: var(--shadow-sm);
}

.kpi-label {
  font-size: 0.76rem;
  text-transform: uppercase;
  letter-spacing: 0.04em;
  color: var(--text-muted);
  font-weight: 700;
}

.kpi-value {
  font-size: 1.65rem;
  font-weight: 800;
  color: var(--text-primary);
  margin: 0.2rem 0;
}

.kpi-sub {
  font-size: 0.78rem;
  color: var(--text-secondary);
}

.grid-2 {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(360px, 1fr));
  gap: 1.1rem;
}

.grid-3 {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(280px, 1fr));
  gap: 1rem;
}

/* Semantic Callout Boxes */
.box-assumption {
  background: var(--color-assumption-bg);
  border-left: 4px solid var(--color-assumption);
  padding: 0.9rem 1.1rem;
  border-radius: var(--radius-sm);
  margin-bottom: 0.85rem;
}

.box-transform {
  background: var(--color-transform-bg);
  border-left: 4px solid var(--color-transform);
  padding: 0.9rem 1.1rem;
  border-radius: var(--radius-sm);
  margin-bottom: 0.85rem;
}

.box-step {
  background: var(--color-step-bg);
  border-left: 4px solid var(--color-step);
  padding: 0.9rem 1.1rem;
  border-radius: var(--radius-sm);
  margin-bottom: 0.85rem;
}

.box-conclusion {
  background: var(--color-conclusion-bg);
  border-left: 4px solid var(--color-conclusion);
  padding: 0.9rem 1.1rem;
  border-radius: var(--radius-sm);
  margin-bottom: 0.85rem;
}

.box-danger {
  background: var(--color-danger-bg);
  border-left: 4px solid var(--color-danger);
  padding: 0.9rem 1.1rem;
  border-radius: var(--radius-sm);
  margin-bottom: 0.85rem;
}

/* Math Blocks & Verbatim Question Display */
.math-block {
  font-family: var(--font-serif);
  font-size: 1.04rem;
  background: var(--bg-elevated);
  padding: 0.85rem 1.1rem;
  border-radius: var(--radius-sm);
  border: 1px solid var(--border-color);
  margin: 0.65rem 0;
  white-space: pre-wrap;
  overflow-x: auto;
}

/* Question Selector Pill Bar */
.q-selector-bar {
  display: flex;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  gap: 0.5rem;
  margin-bottom: 1rem;
  background: var(--bg-surface);
  padding: 0.75rem;
  border-radius: var(--radius-md);
  border: 1px solid var(--border-color);

}

.q-pill {
  padding: 0.5rem 0.9rem;
  border-radius: var(--radius-sm);
  border: 1px solid var(--border-color);
  background: var(--bg-elevated);
  color: var(--text-secondary);
  font-weight: 700;
  font-size: 0.84rem;
  cursor: pointer;
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 0.45rem;
  transition: all var(--transition-speed);
}

.q-pill:hover {
  border-color: var(--accent-primary);
  color: var(--accent-primary);
}

.q-pill.active {
  background: var(--accent-primary);
  color: #ffffff;
  border-color: var(--accent-primary);
}

/* Phase Sub-Tabs */
.phase-tabs {
  display: flex;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  gap: 0.35rem;
  margin-bottom: 1.15rem;
  border-bottom: 2px solid var(--border-color);
  padding-bottom: 0.5rem;
}

.phase-tab {
  padding: 0.5rem 0.9rem;
  border-radius: var(--radius-sm);
  border: 1px solid transparent;
  background: transparent;
  color: var(--text-secondary);
  font-weight: 600;
  font-size: 0.85rem;
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: all var(--transition-speed);
}

.phase-tab:hover {
  background: var(--bg-elevated);
  color: var(--text-primary);
}

.phase-tab.active {
  background: var(--accent-primary-light);
  color: var(--accent-primary);
  border-color: var(--accent-primary);
  font-weight: 700;
}

/* Tables */
.data-table {
  width: 100%;
  border-collapse: collapse;
  font-size: 0.88rem;
  margin: 0.65rem 0;
}

.data-table th, .data-table td {
  border: 1px solid var(--border-color);
  padding: 0.65rem 0.8rem;
  text-align: left;
  vertical-align: top;
}

.data-table th {
  background: var(--bg-elevated);
  font-weight: 700;
  color: var(--text-primary);
}

.data-table tr:nth-child(even) {
  background: rgba(148, 163, 184, 0.05);
}

/* Badges */
.badge {
  display: inline-block;
  padding: 0.18rem 0.55rem;
  border-radius: 999px;
  font-size: 0.73rem;
  font-weight: 700;
}

.badge-blue { background: var(--color-assumption-bg); color: var(--color-assumption); }
.badge-amber { background: var(--color-transform-bg); color: var(--color-transform); }
.badge-purple { background: var(--color-step-bg); color: var(--color-step); }
.badge-green { background: var(--color-conclusion-bg); color: var(--color-conclusion); }
.badge-red { background: var(--color-danger-bg); color: var(--color-danger); }

/* Interactive Visualization Canvas Studio */
.viz-container {
  background: var(--bg-elevated);
  border: 1px solid var(--border-color);
  border-radius: var(--radius-md);
  padding: 1rem;
  margin-bottom: 1rem;
}

.viz-canvas-wrap {
  width: 100%;
  background: var(--bg-surface);
  border: 1px solid var(--border-color);
  border-radius: var(--radius-sm);
  overflow: hidden;
  display: flex;
  justify-content: center;
  margin-bottom: 0.85rem;
}

canvas.math-canvas {
  width: 100%;
  max-width: 920px;
  height: 390px;
  display: block;
}

.viz-controls {
  display: flex;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  gap: 0.65rem;
  align-items: center;
  padding: 0.7rem;
  background: var(--bg-surface);
  border: 1px solid var(--border-color);
  border-radius: var(--radius-sm);
}

.control-group {
  display: flex;
  align-items: center;
  gap: 0.4rem;
  font-size: 0.82rem;
  font-weight: 600;
}

.btn {
  padding: 0.45rem 0.85rem;
  border-radius: var(--radius-sm);
  border: 1px solid var(--border-color);
  background: var(--bg-surface);
  color: var(--text-primary);
  font-weight: 600;
  font-size: 0.83rem;
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: all var(--transition-speed);
}

.btn:hover {
  border-color: var(--accent-primary);
  color: var(--accent-primary);
}

.btn-primary {
  background: var(--accent-primary);
  color: #ffffff;
  border-color: var(--accent-primary);
}

.btn-primary:hover {
  opacity: 0.92;
  color: #ffffff;
}

.btn-success {
  background: var(--color-conclusion);
  color: #ffffff;
  border-color: var(--color-conclusion);
}

.btn-amber {
  background: var(--color-transform);
  color: #ffffff;
  border-color: var(--color-transform);
}

/* Flashcards */
.flashcard-grid {
  display: grid;
  grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fit, minmax(260px, 1fr));
  gap: 0.9rem;
}

.flashcard {
  background: var(--bg-elevated);
  border: 2px solid var(--border-color);
  border-radius: var(--radius-md);
  padding: 1.1rem;
  min-height: 150px;
  cursor: pointer;
  display: flex;
  flex-direction: column;
  justify-content: space-between;
  transition: transform var(--transition-speed), border-color var(--transition-speed);
}

.flashcard:hover {
  border-color: var(--accent-primary);
  transform: translateY(-2px);
}

.flashcard.flipped {
  background: var(--color-conclusion-bg);
  border-color: var(--color-conclusion);
}

/* Flowchart Pills */
.flowchart-row {
  display: flex;
  flex-wrap: wrap;
  gap: 0.45rem;
  align-items: center;
  margin: 0.75rem 0;
}

.flow-node {
  background: var(--bg-elevated);
  border: 1px solid var(--accent-primary);
  color: var(--text-primary);
  padding: 0.45rem 0.75rem;
  border-radius: var(--radius-sm);
  font-size: 0.82rem;
  font-weight: 600;
}

.flow-arrow {
  color: var(--accent-primary);
  font-weight: 800;
  font-size: 1rem;
}

/* Quiz Options */
.quiz-option {
  display: block;
  width: 100%;
  text-align: left;
  padding: 0.65rem 0.9rem;
  margin: 0.4rem 0;
  border-radius: var(--radius-sm);
  border: 1px solid var(--border-color);
  background: var(--bg-surface);
  color: var(--text-primary);
  font-size: 0.88rem;
  cursor: pointer;
  transition: all var(--transition-speed);
}

.quiz-option:hover {
  border-color: var(--accent-primary);
  background: var(--bg-elevated);
}

.quiz-option.correct {
  background: var(--color-conclusion-bg);
  border-color: var(--color-conclusion);
  font-weight: 700;
}

.quiz-option.wrong {
  background: var(--color-danger-bg);
  border-color: var(--color-danger);
}

textarea.scratchpad {
  width: 100%;
  min-height: 95px;
  padding: 0.75rem;
  border-radius: var(--radius-sm);
  border: 1px solid var(--border-color);
  background: var(--bg-surface);
  color: var(--text-primary);
  font-family: var(--font-mono);
  font-size: 0.86rem;
  margin: 0.5rem 0;
}

/* Print Friendly Sheet */
#print-all-container {
  display: none;
}

@media print {
  .top-header, .semantics-bar, #app-root {
    display: none !important;
  }
  #print-all-container {
    display: block !important;
    padding: 1rem;
    color: #000;
    background: #fff;
  }
  .print-q-block {
    page-break-inside: avoid;
    border-bottom: 1px solid #ccc;
    padding-bottom: 1rem;
    margin-bottom: 1rem;
  }
}
</style>
</head>
<body>
<header class="top-header">
  <div class="header-inner">
    <div class="brand-block">
      <div class="brand-course">MAT 421 / MSM 512: Functional Analysis — Problem Sheet 6</div>
      <div class="brand-sub">IISER Thiruvananthapuram • Instructor: Prof. K. R. Arun • TAs: Ashish Kujur, Shraddha Salwahan • Oct 2, 2026</div>
    </div>
    <nav class="global-nav" aria-label="Main Application Views">
      <button class="nav-btn active" id="nav-dashboard" onclick="switchGlobalView('dashboard')">📊 1. Dependency Dashboard</button>
      <button class="nav-btn" id="nav-workspace" onclick="switchGlobalView('workspace')">🎓 2. Interactive Question Tutor (Q1–Q6)</button>
      <button class="nav-btn" id="nav-revision" onclick="switchGlobalView('revision')">🏆 3. Revision & Mock Exam</button>
      <button class="nav-btn urgent-btn" id="nav-last30" onclick="switchGlobalView('last30')">⚡ Last 30 Mins Before Exam</button>
    </nav>
    <div class="utility-controls">
      <select class="util-select" id="order-select" onchange="changeQuestionOrder(this.value)" aria-label="Question Ordering">
        <option value="original">Order: Original (Q1→Q6)</option>
        <option value="pedagogical">Order: Prerequisite (Q1→Q3→Q4→Q2→Q5→Q6)</option>
      </select>
      <button class="util-btn" onclick="toggleTheme()" id="theme-btn">🌙 Dark Mode</button>
      <button class="util-btn" onclick="toggleReducedMotion()" id="motion-btn">🎞️ Motion: On</button>
      <button class="util-btn" onclick="window.print()">🖨️ Print Exam Solutions</button>
      <button class="util-btn" onclick="resetAllProgress()">↺ Reset Progress</button>
    </div>
  </div>
</header>

<div class="semantics-bar">
  <div class="semantics-inner">
    <div class="legend-items">
      <span><strong>Consistent Color Code:</strong></span>
      <span><span class="legend-dot" style="background:var(--color-assumption)"></span>Assumptions & Hypotheses</span>
      <span><span class="legend-dot" style="background:var(--color-transform)"></span>Transformations & Clues</span>
      <span><span class="legend-dot" style="background:var(--color-step)"></span>Intermediate Derivations</span>
      <span><span class="legend-dot" style="background:var(--color-conclusion)"></span>Conclusions & Full-Marks Proofs</span>
    </div>
    <div>
      <span class="badge badge-green">100% Offline Ready (Native Math + Canvas)</span>
      <span class="badge badge-blue" id="katex-status-badge">KaTeX CDN Optional</span>
    </div>
  </div>
</div>

<main class="main-container" id="app-root"></main>
<section id="print-all-container"></section>

<script>
const GLOBAL_DATA = {
  "course": "MAT 421 / MSM 512: Functional Analysis",
  "institution": "Indian Institute of Science Education and Research (IISER) Thiruvananthapuram",
  "sheetTitle": "Problem Sheet 6 — Compact Operators, Closed Graphs & Banach Space Geometry",
  "instructor": "Prof. K. R. Arun",
  "tas": "Ashish Kujur (TA1), Shraddha Salwahan (TA2)",
  "date": "October 2, 2026",
  "legibilityAudit": "All 6 questions and hints across both pages of Problem_Sheet6.pdf were read and verified with 100% clarity. Original wording, numbering (1–6), mathematical notation (ℝ, ℓ₁, ℓ_p, K = K², L₂ ∘ L₁), and instructor hints are preserved verbatim.",
  "pedagogicalSequenceNote": "While you can study in Original Order (Q1 → Q2 → Q3 → Q4 → Q5 → Q6), the Pedagogical Prerequisite Order (Q1 → Q3 → Q4 → Q2 → Q5 → Q6) builds the theory from the ground up: first Closed Graphs in ℝ (Q1), then the Ideal Property of Compact Operators (Q3), then Compact Projections & Riesz's Theorem (Q4), then why Injective Compact Operators cannot have Closed Range via the Bounded Inverse Theorem (Q2), then why Compact Operators cannot be Surjective via the Open Mapping Theorem (Q5), and finally the complete Spectral Characterization of Diagonal Compact Operators on ℓ_p via Finite-Rank Approximation (Q6).",
  "dependencyNodes": [
    {

  "theoremSheet": [
    {
      "name": "Closed Graph Characterization on ℝ (Q1)",
      "hypotheses": "f : ℝ → ℝ is bounded (|f(x)| ≤ M) and G(f) = {(x, f(x))} is closed in ℝ².",
      "conclusion": "f is continuous on ℝ. (Without boundedness or linearity, g(x) = 1/x (x≠0), g(0)=0 is a bijection with closed graph that is discontinuous at 0.)",
      "trigger": "Nonlinear function with closed graph + bounded range."
    },
    {
      "name": "Bounded Inverse Theorem (Banach Isomorphism Theorem — Q2)",
      "hypotheses": "X and Y are BOTH Banach (complete) spaces; T : X → Y is a bounded linear bijection (1-to-1 and onto).",
      "conclusion": "T⁻¹ : Y → X is a bounded (continuous) linear operator.",
      "trigger": "One-to-one operator + closed range Y = Range(T) (since closed subspace of Banach is Banach!)."
    },
    {
      "name": "Open Mapping Theorem (Banach–Schauder — Q5)",
      "hypotheses": "X and Y are BOTH Banach spaces; T : X → Y is a surjective (onto) bounded linear operator.",
      "conclusion": "T is an open map; specifically, ∃ r > 0 such that B_Y(0, r) ⊂ T(B_X(0, 1)).",
      "trigger": "Surjective bounded linear operator between Banach spaces (not necessarily 1-to-1)."
    },
    {
      "name": "Riesz's Theorem on the Closed Unit Ball (Q2, Q4, Q5)",
      "hypotheses": "X is a normed linear space with closed unit ball B_X = {x ∈ X : ‖x‖ ≤ 1}.",
      "conclusion": "B_X is compact in X ⟺ dim(X) < ∞ (equivalently, I_X is compact ⟺ dim(X) < ∞).",
      "trigger": "Whenever an argument shows B_X (or a closed ball in Y or Range(K)) is compact, immediately deduce finite dimensionality!"
    },
    {
      "name": "Ideal & Approximation Properties of Compact Operators (Q3, Q6)",
      "hypotheses": "X, Y, Z are Banach spaces; L₁ : X → Y, L₂ : Y → Z linear.",
      "conclusion": "1. If one of L₁, L₂ is bounded and the other is compact, L₂ ∘ L₁ is compact.\n2. Every bounded finite-rank operator (dim Range(T) < ∞) is compact.\n3. If L_n ∈ K(X, Y) and ‖L - L_n‖ → 0 in operator norm (with Y Banach), then L ∈ K(X, Y).",
      "trigger": "Compositions of operators (Q3) or diagonal/integral operators approximated by finite-rank truncations L_n (Q6)."
    }
  ],
  "proofPatterns": [
    {
      "pattern": "Pattern 1: Proving Range(K) is Finite-Dimensional or Non-Closed",
      "usedIn": "Q2, Q4, Q5",
      "steps": "Assume Range(K) is closed in Banach Y ⇒ Z = Range(K) is Banach ⇒ apply BIT (if 1-1) or OMT (if onto Z) or K|_Z = I_Z (if K²=K) ⇒ deduce closed unit ball B_Z is compact ⇒ by Riesz's Theorem, dim Range(K) < ∞!"
    },
    {
      "pattern": "Pattern 2: Proving an Operator on ℓ_p is Compact",
      "usedIn": "Q5, Q6",
      "steps": "Define finite-rank truncations L_n(x) = (λ₁x₁, ..., λ_n x_n, 0, ...) ⇒ note dim Range(L_n) ≤ n < ∞ so L_n is compact ⇒ prove UNIFORM operator norm convergence ‖L - L_n‖ ≤ sup_{k > n} |λ_k| → 0."
    },
    {
      "pattern": "Pattern 3: Proving an Operator on ℓ_p is NOT Compact",
      "usedIn": "Q6 (and variations)",
      "steps": "Feed in the bounded sequence of unit basis vectors (e_{k_ℓ}) (with ‖e_{k_ℓ}‖_p = 1) and show pairwise separation ‖L(e_{k_ℓ}) - L(e_{k_m})‖_p ≥ c > 0 for all ℓ ≠ m so no subsequence can be Cauchy."
    }
  ],

    "examAnswer": {
      "fullProof": "Part 1: Let f : ℝ → ℝ be bounded (|f(x)| ≤ M ∀x ∈ ℝ) with a closed graph G(f) in ℝ². Fix x ∈ ℝ and let xₙ → x.\nSuppose for contradiction that f(xₙ) ↛ f(x). Then there exist ε > 0 and a subsequence (xₙₖ) such that |f(xₙₖ) - f(x)| ≥ ε for all k.\nSince |f(xₙₖ)| ≤ M, by the Bolzano–Weierstrass Theorem there is a further subsequence f(xₙₖⱼ) → y ∈ ℝ. Then (xₙₖⱼ, f(xₙₖⱼ)) → (x, y) in ℝ². Since G(f) is closed, (x, y) ∈ G(f), so y = f(x), contradicting |y - f(x)| ≥ ε > 0. Thus f(xₙ) → f(x), and f is continuous.\n\nPart 2: Define g : ℝ → ℝ by g(x) = 1/x for x ≠ 0 and g(0) = 0.\n1. Bijection: For all x ∈ ℝ, g(g(x)) = x (since g(g(0))=0 and g(1/x)=x for x≠0). Thus g ∘ g = id_ℝ, so g is one-to-one and onto.\n2. Discontinuity: For xₙ = 1/n → 0, g(xₙ) = n → +∞ ≠ 0 = g(0), so g is discontinuous at 0.\n3. Closed Graph: Suppose (xₙ, g(xₙ)) → (x, y) in ℝ².\n   • If x ≠ 0, then xₙ ≠ 0 for large n, and continuity of t ↦ 1/t on ℝ\\{0} gives y = lim(1/xₙ) = 1/x = g(x).\n   • If x = 0, then xₙ → 0 and g(xₙ) → y ∈ ℝ. If xₙ ≠ 0 for infinitely many n, along that subsequence |g(xₙₖ)| = 1/|xₙₖ| → ∞, contradicting g(xₙ) → y ∈ ℝ. Thus xₙ = 0 for all large n, so g(xₙ) = 0 → 0 and y = 0 = g(0).\nHence (x, y) ∈ G(g) in all cases, so G(g) is closed. ∎",
      "quickRevision": "• Part 1: Let xₙ → x.       "fullProof": "Suppose for contradiction that Y := Range(K) is closed in X.\n1. Since X is a Banach space and Y is a closed subspace of X, Y is a Banach space.\n2. Since K is compact, it is bounded; since K is one-to-one, the corestriction K̃ : X → Y, K̃x = Kx, is a bounded linear bijection between Banach spaces.\n3. By the Bounded Inverse Theorem, K̃⁻¹ : Y → X is a bounded (continuous) linear operator.\n4. Let B_X be the closed unit ball of X. Since K is compact and Y is closed, Cl_Y(K(B_X)) is compact in Y. By continuity of K̃⁻¹, K̃⁻¹(Cl_Y(K(B_X))) is compact in X. Since B_X = K̃⁻¹(K(B_X)) ⊂ K̃⁻¹(Cl_Y(K(B_X))) is a closed subset of a compact set, B_X is compact in X.\n5. By Riesz's Theorem, compactness of B_X implies dim(X) < ∞, contradicting that X is infinite-dimensional. Hence Range(K) cannot be closed. ∎",

      "intuition": "Think of a bounded operator as a 'controlled magnifier' (stretches balls by at most a finite factor ‖L‖) and a compact operator as a 'dimension compactor' (crushes bounded sets into precompact sets).\n• Case 1 (X --bounded L₁--> Y --compact L₂--> Z): L₁ stretches a bounded set B ⊂ X into a bounded set L₁(B) ⊂ Y, which L₂ then crushes into a precompact set in Z.\n• Case 2 (X --compact L₁--> Y --continuous L₂--> Z): L₁ crushes B ⊂ X into a set with compact closure K = Cl_Y(L₁(B)), and continuous L₂ maps K into a compact container L₂(K) ⊂ Z!",

    "examAnswer": {
      "fullProof": "Since L₁ and L₂ are linear, L₂ ∘ L₁ : X → Z is linear. Let B ⊂ X be any bounded subset (‖x‖_X ≤ R ∀x ∈ B).\n\nCase 1 (L₁ continuous, L₂ compact): Since L₁ is bounded, ‖L₁x‖_Y ≤ ‖L₁‖R for all x ∈ B, so L₁(B) is a bounded subset of Y. Since L₂ : Y → Z is compact, Cl_Z((L₂ ∘ L₁)(B)) = Cl_Z(L₂(L₁(B))) is compact in Z.\n\nCase 2 (L₁ compact, L₂ continuous): Since L₁ is compact and B is bounded, K := Cl_Y(L₁(B)) is compact in Y. Since L₂ : Y → Z is continuous, L₂(K) is compact in Z, and hence closed in the Banach space Z. Since (L₂ ∘ L₁)(B) = L₂(L₁(B)) ⊂ L₂(K), taking closures in Z yields Cl_Z((L₂ ∘ L₁)(B)) ⊂ L₂(K). As a closed subset of the compact set L₂(K), Cl_Z((L₂ ∘ L₁)(B)) is compact in Z.\nThus, in both cases, L₂ ∘ L₁ is a compact linear operator. ∎",
      "quickRevision": "• Case 1: B bounded in X ⇒ L₁(B) bounded in Y (‖L₁x‖ ≤ ‖L₁‖R) ⇒ Cl_Z(L₂(L₁(B))) compact in Z.\n• Case 2: B bounded in X ⇒ K = Cl_Y(L₁(B)) compact in Y ⇒ L₂(K) compact (hence closed) in Z ⇒ Cl_Z(L₂(L₁(B))) ⊂ L₂(K) is closed in compact, hence compact. ∎",

    "connectionsToOtherQuestions": "Directly illustrates Riesz's Theorem on Range(K): because K = K², K acts as the IDENTITY on Range(K), so compactness of K forces the closed unit ball of Range(K) to be compact, hence dim Range(K) < ∞! Connects to Q2, Q5, and Q6.",
    "originalText": "4. Let X be a Banach space and let K : X → X be a compact linear operator such that K = K², i.e., K(x) = K(K(x)) for all x ∈ X. Prove that Range(K) is finite-dimensional.",
    "originalHint": "Hint. Show first that Range(K) = Ker(I - K) is closed. Observe that K restricts to the identity operator on Range(K). Since this restriction is compact, what does this imply about the closed unit ball of Range(K)?",
    "classroom": {
      "motivation": "A linear operator satisfying K² = K is a **projection** onto Y = Range(K): once a vector lands in Range(K), applying K again leaves it completely untouched (Ky = y for all y ∈ Range(K))! On the other hand, a compact operator must 'squash' infinite-dimensional balls. How can an operator simultaneously leave every vector in Range(K) untouched (acting as the identity I_Y on Y = Range(K)) AND be compact? Only if the subspace Y = Range(K) is FINITE-DIMENSIONAL!",

      "fullProof": "Step 1: We show that Range(K) = Ker(I - K) and hence is closed in X.\n• If y ∈ Range(K), then y = K(x) for some x ∈ X. Since K = K², we have\n      K(y) = K(K(x)) = K²(x) = K(x) = y   ⟹   (I - K)(y) = y - K(y) = 0,\n  so y ∈ Ker(I - K). Thus Range(K) ⊂ Ker(I - K).\n• Conversely, if y ∈ Ker(I - K), then y - K(y) = 0, so y = K(y) ∈ Range(K). Thus Ker(I - K) ⊂ Range(K).\nHence Y := Range(K) = Ker(I - K). Since K is compact, K is continuous (bounded), so I - K : X → X is continuous. Therefore Y = Ker(I - K) = (I - K)⁻¹({0}) is a closed linear subspace of X.\n\nStep 2: We show that the closed unit ball of Y = Range(K) is compact.\nFor every y ∈ Y = Ker(I - K), we have K(y) = y, so the restriction K|_Y : Y → Y is the identity operator I_Y on Y. Let B_Y = {y ∈ Y : ‖y‖ ≤ 1} be the closed unit ball of Y. Since Y is closed in X, B_Y is closed in X. Because K|_Y = I_Y, we have\n    B_Y = K(B_Y).\nSince B_Y is a bounded subset of X and K is a compact operator, Cl_X(K(B_Y)) is compact in X. Because B_Y = K(B_Y) is already closed in X, B_Y = Cl_X(K(B_Y)) is compact!\n\nStep 3: By Riesz's Theorem, a normed linear space whose closed unit ball is compact must be finite-dimensional. Therefore, Range(K) is finite-dimensional. ∎",

    "examAnswer": {
      "fullProof": "(⇐) Suppose lim_{k→∞} λ_k = 0. For each n ∈ ℕ, define the truncated linear operator L_n : ℓ_p → ℓ_p by\n    L_n(x₁, x₂, x₃, ...) := (λ₁x₁, λ₂x₂, ..., λ_n x_n, 0, 0, ...).\n1. Each L_n is a bounded linear operator (with ‖L_n‖ ≤ sup_k |λ_k| < ∞) whose range Range(L_n) ⊂ span{e₁, ..., e_n} is at most n-dimensional. Since every finite-rank bounded linear operator is compact, L_n is compact for every n ∈ ℕ.\n2. For any x = (x_k) ∈ ℓ_p, we have\n       ‖(L - L_n)(x)‖_p^p = ∑_{k=n+1}^∞ |λ_k x_k|^p ≤ (sup_{k > n} |λ_k|)^p ∑_{k=n+1}^∞ |x_k|^p ≤ (sup_{k > n} |λ_k|)^p ‖x‖_p^p.\n   Taking p-th roots yields ‖(L - L_n)(x)‖_p ≤ (sup_{k > n} |λ_k|) ‖x‖_p for all x ∈ ℓ_p, so\n       ‖L - L_n‖ ≤ sup_{k > n} |λ_k|.\n3. Since lim_{k→∞} λ_k = 0, we have lim_{n→∞} (sup_{k > n} |λ_k|) = 0, and hence ‖L - L_n‖ → 0 as n → ∞. Because ℓ_p is a Banach space, the limit of a sequence of compact operators in the operator norm is compact. Thus L is compact.\n\n(⇒) Conversely, suppose lim_{k→∞} λ_k ≠ 0 (or does not exist). We show L is not compact.\n1. Since λ_k ↛ 0, there exist ε > 0 and a subsequence (λ_{k_ℓ})_{ℓ=1}^∞ (with k₁ < k₂ < k₃ < ···) such that\n       |λ_{k_ℓ}| > ε   for all ℓ ∈ ℕ.\n2. Consider the standard unit vectors e_{k_ℓ} ∈ ℓ_p (having 1 in the k_ℓ-th position and 0 elsewhere). Then ‖e_{k_ℓ}‖_p = 1 for all ℓ, so (e_{k_ℓ})_{ℓ=1}^∞ is a bounded sequence in ℓ_p.\n3. However, L(e_{k_ℓ}) = λ_{k_ℓ} e_{k_ℓ}, so for any ℓ ≠ m (which implies k_ℓ ≠ k_m),\n       ‖L(e_{k_ℓ}) - L(e_{k_m})‖_p = ‖λ_{k_ℓ} e_{k_ℓ} - λ_{k_m} e_{k_m}‖_p = (|λ_{k_ℓ}|^p + |λ_{k_m}|^p)^{1/p} > (ε^p + ε^p)^{1/p} = 2^{1/p} ε > 0.\n   Because the distance between any two distinct terms is > 2^{1/p}ε, the sequence (L(e_{k_ℓ}))_{ℓ=1}^∞ has no Cauchy subsequence, and hence no convergent subsequence in ℓ_p. Therefore, L is not compact. ∎",
      "quickRevision": "• (⇐) Assume λ_k → 0. Let L_n(x) = (λ₁x₁, ..., λ_n x_n, 0, ...). Since dim Range(L_n) ≤ n < ∞, each L_n is compact. Moreover, ‖(L - L_n)x‖_p = (∑_{k>n}|λ_k x_k|^p)^{1/p} ≤ (sup_{k>n}|λ_k|)‖x‖_p, so ‖L - L_n‖ ≤ sup_{k>n}|λ_k| → 0; since ℓ_p is Banach, L is compact.\n• (⇒) If λ_k ↛ 0, ∃ ε > 0 and k₁ < k₂ < ··· with |λ_{k_ℓ}| > ε. Then (e_{k_ℓ}) is bounded (‖e_{k_ℓ}‖_p = 1), but for ℓ ≠ m, ‖Le_{k_ℓ} - Le_{k_m}‖_p = (|λ_{k_ℓ}|^p + |λ_{k_m}|^p)^{1/p} > 2^{1/p}ε, so (Le_{k_ℓ}) has no convergent subsequence. ∎",
      "markingPerspective": {
        "estimatedTotal": "10 Marks (5 marks (⇐) + 5 marks (⇒))",
        "essential": [
          "[2 marks] Defining L_n and stating that Range(L_n) is finite-dimensional, so L_n is compact.",
          "[3 marks] Proving the operator-norm bound ‖L - L_n‖ ≤ sup_{k > n} |λ_k| → 0 and citing that the operator-norm limit of compact operators on a Banach space is compact.",
          "[2 marks] Extracting ε > 0 and a subsequence |λ_{k_ℓ}| > ε when lim λ_k ≠ 0.",
          "[3 marks] Evaluating L on the unit vectors (e_{k_ℓ}) and proving ‖L(e_{k_ℓ}) - L(e_{k_m})‖_p > 2^{1/p}ε (or coordinate decay contradiction) so no subsequence converges."
        ],
        "omissionsToLoseMarks": [
          "Showing only pointwise convergence ‖(L - L_n)x‖_p → 0 instead of operator-norm convergence ‖L - L_n‖ → 0 (-2.5 marks).",
          "Showing only ‖L(e_{k_ℓ})‖_p > ε without showing pairwise separation ‖L(e_{k_ℓ}) - L(e_{k_m})‖_p > 2^{1/p}ε (-2 marks)."
        ],
        "commonMistakes": [
          "Claiming ‖L - L_n‖ = (∑_{k>n} |λ_k|^p)^{1/p} (wrong! The operator norm of a diagonal operator on ℓ_p is the SUPREMUM norm sup_k |λ_k|, not the ℓ_p norm!)."
        ]
      }
    },
    "memoryPalace": {
      "skeleton": [
        "1. (⇐) Define finite-rank truncation L_n(x) = (λ₁x₁, ..., λ_n x_n, 0, ...) ⇒ dim Range(L_n) ≤ n < ∞ ⇒ L_n is compact.",
        "2. Bound tail: ‖(L - L_n)x‖_p^p = ∑_{k>n}|λ_k x_k|^p ≤ (sup_{k>n}|λ_k|)^p ‖x‖_p^p ⇒ ‖L - L_n‖ ≤ sup_{k>n}|λ_k| → 0 ⇒ L is compact.",
        "3. (⇒) Suppose λ_k ↛ 0 ⇒ ∃ ε > 0 and k₁ < k₂ < ··· with |λ_{k_ℓ}| > ε.",
        "4. Test unit vectors e_{k_ℓ} (‖e_{k_ℓ}‖_p = 1): for ℓ ≠ m, ‖Le_{k_ℓ} - Le_{k_m}‖_p = (|λ_{k_ℓ}|^p + |λ_{k_m}|^p)^{1/p} > 2^{1/p}ε ⇒ no Cauchy subsequence!"
      ],
      "anchors": [
        "Anchor 1 ('Supremum Tail Norm'): Operator norm of a diagonal map is ALWAYS the supremum: ‖L - L_n‖ = sup_{k > n} |λ_k|.",

      ],

If infinitely many sliders stay above volume ε, tapping those keys e_{k_ℓ} produces pure tones on different axes that stay 2^{1/p}ε apart forever!",

        "hypothesisChange": "Contrasts a compact residual branch K with the skip-connected operator I + K (Fredholm operator!).",
        "hints": [
          "Notice T(x_k) = ((1 + λ_k)x_k) has multipliers μ_k = 1 + λ_k.",
          "What is lim_{k→∞} μ_k?"
        ],
        "solution": "Since λ_k → 0, the ResNet multipliers satisfy lim_{k→∞} μ_k = lim_{k→∞} (1 + λ_k) = 1 ≠ 0! By Question 6, T = I + K is NOT compact—and that skip connection I is precisely what prevents high-frequency signal collapse (and makes Range(I + K) closed by the Fredholm Alternative)!"
      }

];

// --- STATE MANAGEMENT & LOCALSTORAGE PERSISTENCE ---
const STORAGE_KEY = 'fa_iisertvm_ps6_state_v1';

const defaultState = {
  globalView: 'dashboard', // 'dashboard' | 'workspace' | 'revision' | 'last30'
  orderMode: 'original',   // 'original' | 'pedagogical'
  currentQId: 'q1',
  currentTab: 'classroom', // 'classroom' | 'decoder' | 'viz' | 'solution' | 'exam' | 'memory' | 'practice'
  selectedDepNode: 'compact_def',
  theme: 'light',
  reducedMotion: false,
  // Mastery & progress tracking (Viewing a solution NEVER auto-marks Mastered!)
  mastery: { q1: 'not_started', q2: 'not_started', q3: 'not_started', q4: 'not_started', q5: 'not_started', q6: 'not_started' },
  bookmarks: { q1: false, q2: false, q3: false, q4: false, q5: false, q6: false },
  revealedSteps: { q1: 1, q2: 1, q3: 1, q4: 1, q5: 1, q6: 1 },
  selectedMethod: { q1: 'methodB', q2: 'methodB', q3: 'methodB', q4: 'methodB', q5: 'methodB', q6: 'methodB' },
  compareAllMode: { q1: false, q2: false, q3: false, q4: false, q5: false, q6: false },
  examMode: { q1: 'full', q2: 'full', q3: 'full', q4: 'full', q5: 'full', q6: 'full' },
  checkpointAnswers: {},     // key: `${qId}_cp_${idx}` -> selectedIndex
  errorDetAnswers: {},       // key: `${qId}_err` -> selectedIndex
  fillBlankStatus: {},       // key: `${qId}_fill` -> boolean
  recallHintLevel: { q1: 0, q2: 0, q3: 0, q4: 0, q5: 0, q6: 0 },
  flippedCards: {},          // key: `${qId}_fc_${idx}` -> boolean
  srSchedule: { q1: 'Immediate', q2: 'Immediate', q3: 'Immediate', q4: 'Immediate', q5: 'Immediate', q6: 'Immediate' },
  srCustomDays: { q1: 3, q2: 3, q3: 3, q4: 3, q5: 3, q6: 3 },
  practiceHints: {},         // key: `${qId}_pr_${idx}` -> count (0..3)
  practiceRevealed: {},      // key: `${qId}_pr_${idx}` -> boolean
  practiceGrade: {},         // key: `${qId}_pr_${idx}` -> 'correct' | 'hints' | 'review'
  mockRubric: {},            // key: `${mId}_${rIdx}` -> boolean
  mockTimerSecs: 2700,
  mockTimerRunning: false
};

let state = loadState();
let mockTimerInterval = null;

function loadState() {
  try {
    const raw = localStorage.getItem(STORAGE_KEY);
    if (!raw) return JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(defaultState));
    const parsed = JSON.parse(raw);
    return Object.assign(JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(defaultState)), parsed);
  } catch (e) {
    return JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(defaultState));
  }
}

function saveState() {
  try {
    localStorage.setItem(STORAGE_KEY, JSON.stringify(state));
  } catch (e) {}
}

function resetAllProgress() {
  if (!confirm('Reset all learning progress, quiz answers, and spaced repetition schedules?')) return;
  state = JSON.parse(JSON.stringify(defaultState));
  saveState();
  applyPreferences();
  renderApp();
}

function getOrderedQuestions() {
  const copy = [...QUESTIONS];
  if (state.orderMode === 'pedagogical') {
    copy.sort((a, b) => a.pedagogicalOrder - b.pedagogicalOrder);
  } else {
    copy.sort((a, b) => a.number - b.number);
  }
  return copy;
}

function getCurrentQuestion() {
  return QUESTIONS.find(q => q.id === state.currentQId) || QUESTIONS[0];
}

function escapeHtml(str) {
  if (str === undefined || str === null) return '';
  return String(str)
    .replace(/&/g, '&amp;')
    .replace(/</g, '&lt;')
    .replace(/>/g, '&gt;');
}

// --- GLOBAL CONTROLS ---
function applyPreferences() {
  document.documentElement.setAttribute('data-theme', state.theme);
  document.documentElement.setAttribute('data-reduced-motion', state.reducedMotion ? 'true' : 'false');
  const tBtn = document.getElementById('theme-btn');
  if (tBtn) tBtn.textContent = state.theme === 'dark' ? '☀️ Light Mode' : '🌙 Dark Mode';
  const mBtn = document.getElementById('motion-btn');
  if (mBtn) mBtn.textContent = state.reducedMotion ? '🎞️ Motion: Reduced' : '🎞️ Motion: On';
  const oSel = document.getElementById('order-select');
  if (oSel) oSel.value = state.orderMode;
}

function toggleTheme() {
  state.theme = state.theme === 'dark' ? 'light' : 'dark';
  saveState();
  applyPreferences();
  if (state.globalView === 'workspace' && state.currentTab === 'viz') {
    initOrUpdateCanvas();
  }
}

function toggleReducedMotion() {
  state.reducedMotion = !state.reducedMotion;
  saveState();
  applyPreferences();
}

function changeQuestionOrder(val) {
  state.orderMode = val;
  saveState();
  renderApp();
}

function switchGlobalView(viewName) {
  state.globalView = viewName;
  saveState();
  ['dashboard', 'workspace', 'revision', 'last30'].forEach(v => {
    const btn = document.getElementById('nav-' + v);
    if (btn) btn.classList.toggle('active', v === viewName);
  });
  renderApp();
}

function selectQuestion(qId, optionalTab) {
  state.currentQId = qId;
  if (optionalTab) state.currentTab = optionalTab;
  if (state.mastery[qId] === 'not_started') {
    state.mastery[qId] = 'in_progress';
  }
  state.globalView = 'workspace';
  saveState();
  switchGlobalView('workspace');
}

function switchPhaseTab(tabName) {
  state.currentTab = tabName;
  saveState();
  renderApp();
}

function setMastery(qId, status) {
  state.mastery[qId] = status;
  saveState();
  renderApp();
}

function toggleBookmark(qId) {
  state.bookmarks[qId] = !state.bookmarks[qId];
  saveState();
  renderApp();
}

function masteryBadgeHtml(status) {
  const map = {
    'not_started': '<span class="badge badge-blue">Not Started</span>',
    'in_progress': '<span class="badge badge-purple">In Progress</span>',
    'needs_revision': '<span class="badge badge-amber">Needs Revision</span>',
    'mastered': '<span class="badge badge-green">✓ Mastered</span>'
  };
  return map[status] || map['not_started'];
}

// --- MAIN RENDER DISPATCHER ---
function renderApp() {
  stopVizAnimation();
  const root = document.getElementById('app-root');
  if (!root) return;

  if (state.globalView === 'dashboard') {
    root.innerHTML = renderDashboardView();
  } else if (state.globalView === 'workspace') {
    root.innerHTML = renderWorkspaceView();
    if (state.currentTab === 'viz') {
      setTimeout(() => initOrUpdateCanvas(), 20);
    }
  } else if (state.globalView === 'revision') {
    root.innerHTML = renderRevisionView();
  } else if (state.globalView === 'last30') {
    root.innerHTML = renderLast30View();
  }
  renderPrintAllContainer();
}

// --- VIEW 1: PHASE 1 DASHBOARD ---
function renderDashboardView() {
  const ordered = getOrderedQuestions();
  const masteredCount = Object.values(state.mastery).filter(s => s === 'mastered').length;
  const revisionCount = Object.values(state.mastery).filter(s => s === 'needs_revision').length;
  const inProgCount = Object.values(state.mastery).filter(s => s === 'in_progress').length;
  const totalTime = QUESTIONS.reduce((acc, q) => acc + q.estTimeMins, 0);

  let cpDone = 0;
  let cpTotal = 0;
  QUESTIONS.forEach(q => {
    q.classroom.checkpoints.forEach((_, idx) => {
      cpTotal++;
      if (state.checkpointAnswers[`${q.id}_cp_${idx}`] === q.classroom.checkpoints[idx].correctIndex) cpDone++;
    });
  });

  const activeNode = GLOBAL_DATA.dependencyNodes.find(n => n.id === state.selectedDepNode) || GLOBAL_DATA.dependencyNodes[0];

  return `
    <div class="card box-assumption">
      <div class="card-title">
        <span>📜 Phase 1: Complete Analysis of ${escapeHtml(GLOBAL_DATA.sheetTitle)}</span>
        <span class="badge badge-green">Legibility & Integrity Verified: 6/6 Questions</span>
      </div>
      <p style="font-size:0.9rem; margin-bottom:0.45rem;"><strong>Verbatim Extraction Audit:</strong> ${escapeHtml(GLOBAL_DATA.legibilityAudit)}</p>
      <p style="font-size:0.86rem; color:var(--text-secondary);"><strong>Offline & Rendering Disclosure:</strong> All 6 interactive HTML5 Canvas simulations, multi-method proof engines, quizzes, flashcards, and progress tracking operate <strong>100% offline</strong> using native HTML/Unicode mathematical formatting. If internet access is present, KaTeX CDN is loaded optionally.</p>
    </div>

    <div class="kpi-grid">
      <div class="kpi-card">
        <div class="kpi-label">Total Questions</div>
        <div class="kpi-value">6</div>
        <div class="kpi-sub">MAT 421 / MSM 512 Sheet 6</div>
      </div>
      <div class="kpi-card" style="border-left-color:var(--color-conclusion);">
        <div class="kpi-label">Questions Mastered</div>
        <div class="kpi-value">${masteredCount} / 6</div>
        <div class="kpi-sub">${inProgCount} in progress (self-verified)</div>
      </div>
      <div class="kpi-card" style="border-left-color:var(--color-transform);">
        <div class="kpi-label">Requiring Revision</div>
        <div class="kpi-value">${revisionCount}</div>
        <div class="kpi-sub">Flagged for spaced review</div>
      </div>
      <div class="kpi-card" style="border-left-color:var(--color-step);">
        <div class="kpi-label">Checkpoints Verified</div>
        <div class="kpi-value">${cpDone} / ${cpTotal}</div>
        <div class="kpi-sub">Prerequisite mastery checks</div>
      </div>
      <div class="kpi-card">
        <div class="kpi-label">Estimated Study Time</div>
        <div class="kpi-value">${totalTime} min</div>
        <div class="kpi-sub">~2.5 hrs complete mastery</div>
      </div>
    </div>

    <div class="grid-2">
      <div class="card">
        <div class="card-title">
          <span>🕸️ Interactive Concept & Theorem Dependency Map</span>
          <span class="badge badge-purple">Click any pillar node</span>
        </div>
        <p style="font-size:0.84rem; color:var(--text-secondary); margin-bottom:0.75rem;">Click a foundational concept below to highlight which questions in Problem Sheet 6 depend on it:</p>
        <div style="display:flex; flex-wrap:wrap; gap:0.45rem; margin-bottom:0.85rem;">
          ${GLOBAL_DATA.dependencyNodes.map(n => `
            <button class="btn ${state.selectedDepNode === n.id ? 'btn-primary' : ''}" onclick="selectDepNode('${n.id}')">
              ${escapeHtml(n.label)} (Q${n.questions.join(', Q')})
            </button>
          `).join('')}
        </div>
        <div class="box-step">
          <div style="font-weight:700; margin-bottom:0.25rem;">${escapeHtml(activeNode.label)} — <span class="badge badge-purple">${escapeHtml(activeNode.category)}</span></div>
          <p style="font-size:0.88rem; margin-bottom:0.4rem;">${escapeHtml(activeNode.summary)}</p>
          <div style="font-size:0.83rem;"><strong>Directly Powers Questions:</strong>
            ${activeNode.questions.map(qNum => `<button class="btn" style="padding:0.2rem 0.55rem; margin-left:0.3rem;" onclick="selectQuestion('q${qNum}')">Jump to Q${qNum} →</button>`).join('')}
          </div>
        </div>
        ${renderDependencySvg(activeNode)}
      </div>

      <div class="card">
        <div class="card-title">
          <span>🧭 Recommended Learning Sequence vs. Original Numbering</span>
          <span class="badge badge-amber">Order: ${state.orderMode === 'pedagogical' ? 'Prerequisite' : 'Original'}</span>
        </div>
        <p style="font-size:0.86rem; color:var(--text-secondary); margin-bottom:0.75rem;">${escapeHtml(GLOBAL_DATA.pedagogicalSequenceNote)}</p>
        <div style="display:flex; gap:0.5rem; margin-bottom:0.85rem;">
          <button class="btn ${state.orderMode === 'original' ? 'btn-primary' : ''}" onclick="changeQuestionOrder('original')">1–6 Original Numbering</button>
          <button class="btn ${state.orderMode === 'pedagogical' ? 'btn-primary' : ''}" onclick="changeQuestionOrder('pedagogical')">Prerequisite Sequence (Q1→Q3→Q4→Q2→Q5→Q6)</button>
        </div>
        <div class="flowchart-row">
          <span class="flow-node" onclick="selectQuestion('q1')" style="cursor:pointer">Q1: Closed Graph on ℝ</span>
          <span class="flow-arrow">→</span>
          <span class="flow-node" onclick="selectQuestion('q3')" style="cursor:pointer">Q3: Ideal L₂∘L₁ Compact</span>
          <span class="flow-arrow">→</span>
          <span class="flow-node" onclick="selectQuestion('q4')" style="cursor:pointer">Q4: Projection K=K²</span>
          <span class="flow-arrow">→</span>
          <span class="flow-node" onclick="selectQuestion('q2')" style="cursor:pointer">Q2: 1-1 Non-Closed Range</span>
          <span class="flow-arrow">→</span>
          <span class="flow-node" onclick="selectQuestion('q5')" style="cursor:pointer">Q5: Non-Surjective & ℓ₁</span>
          <span class="flow-arrow">→</span>
          <span class="flow-node" onclick="selectQuestion('q6')" style="cursor:pointer">Q6: Diagonal ℓ_p ⇔ λ_k→0</span>
        </div>
      </div>
    </div>

    <div class="card">
      <div class="card-title">
        <span>📋 Complete Question Dependency, Technique & Mastery Matrix</span>
        <span style="font-size:0.8rem; color:var(--text-muted);">Click any question to launch the 7-Phase Interactive Tutor</span>
      </div>
      <div style="overflow-x:auto;">
        <table class="data-table">
          <thead>
            <tr>
              <th>Orig #</th>
              <th>Pedag #</th>
              <th>Topic & Core Objective</th>
              <th>Key Theorems & Proof Technique</th>
              <th>Difficulty & Est. Time</th>
              <th>Mastery Status</th>
              <th>Action</th>
            </tr>
          </thead>
          <tbody>
            ${ordered.map(q => {
              const highlighted = activeNode.questions.includes(q.number);
              return `
                <tr style="${highlighted ? 'background:var(--color-assumption-bg);' : ''}">
                  <td><strong>Q${q.number}</strong> ${state.bookmarks[q.id] ? '⭐' : ''}</td>
                  <td>Step ${q.pedagogicalOrder}</td>
                  <td>
                    <div style="font-weight:700; color:var(--accent-primary); cursor:pointer;" onclick="selectQuestion('${q.id}')">${escapeHtml(q.title)}</div>
                    <div style="font-size:0.8rem; color:var(--text-secondary); margin-top:0.2rem;">${escapeHtml(q.connectionsToOtherQuestions)}</div>
                  </td>
                  <td>
                    <div style="font-size:0.81rem;"><strong>Theorems:</strong> ${escapeHtml(q.theoremsUsed.join('; '))}</div>
                    <div style="font-size:0.79rem; color:var(--text-muted); margin-top:0.2rem;"><strong>Exam Length:</strong> ${escapeHtml(q.examLength)}</div>
                  </td>
                  <td>
                    <span class="badge ${q.difficulty.includes('Hard') ? 'badge-red' : q.difficulty.includes('Medium') ? 'badge-amber' : 'badge-green'}">${escapeHtml(q.difficulty)}</span>
                    <div style="font-size:0.8rem; margin-top:0.25rem;">⏱️ ${q.estTimeMins} mins</div>
                  </td>
                  <td>
                    ${masteryBadgeHtml(state.mastery[q.id])}
                    <div style="margin-top:0.35rem;">
                      <select class="util-select" style="font-size:0.74rem; padding:0.2rem;" onchange="setMastery('${q.id}', this.value)">
                        <option value="not_started" ${state.mastery[q.id]==='not_started'?'selected':''}>Not Started</option>
                        <option value="in_progress" ${state.mastery[q.id]==='in_progress'?'selected':''}>In Progress</option>
                        <option value="needs_revision" ${state.mastery[q.id]==='needs_revision'?'selected':''}>Needs Revision</option>
                        <option value="mastered" ${state.mastery[q.id]==='mastered'?'selected':''}>Mastered</option>
                      </select>
                    </div>
                  </td>
                  <td>
                    <button class="btn btn-primary" onclick="selectQuestion('${q.id}', 'classroom')">Study Q${q.number} →</button>
                  </td>
                </tr>
              `;
            }).join('')}
          </tbody>
        </table>
      </div>
    </div>
  `;
}

function selectDepNode(nodeId) {
  state.selectedDepNode = nodeId;
  saveState();
  renderApp();
}

function renderDependencySvg(activeNode) {
  const qs = [1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6];
  return `
    <svg viewBox="0 0 680 155" style="width:100%; height:auto; background:var(--bg-elevated); border-radius:8px; border:1px solid var(--border-color); margin-top:0.4rem;">
      <rect x="200" y="12" width="280" height="36" rx="8" fill="#1d4ed8" />
      <text x="340" y="35" text-anchor="middle" fill="#ffffff" font-size="12" font-weight="bold">${escapeHtml(activeNode.label)}</text>
      ${qs.map((qNum, i) => {
        const cx = 65 + i * 110;
        const cy = 115;
        const isLinked = activeNode.questions.includes(qNum);
        return `
          <line x1="340" y1="48" x2="${cx}" y2="${cy - 22}" stroke="${isLinked ? '#d97706' : '#94a3b8'}" stroke-width="${isLinked ? '3' : '1'}" stroke-dasharray="${isLinked ? 'none' : '4,4'}" />
          <g style="cursor:pointer" onclick="selectQuestion('q${qNum}')">
            <rect x="${cx - 42}" y="${cy - 22}" width="84" height="40" rx="8" fill="${isLinked ? '#059669' : '#475569'}" />
            <text x="${cx}" y="${cy + 3}" text-anchor="middle" fill="#ffffff" font-size="13" font-weight="bold">Question ${qNum}</text>
          </g>
        `;
      }).join('')}
    </svg>
  `;
}

// --- VIEW 2: QUESTION TUTOR WORKSPACE (PHASES 2 TO 8) ---
function renderWorkspaceView() {
  const ordered = getOrderedQuestions();
  const q = getCurrentQuestion();
  const curIdx = ordered.findIndex(item => item.id === q.id);
  const prevQ = curIdx > 0 ? ordered[curIdx - 1] : null;
  const nextQ = curIdx < ordered.length - 1 ? ordered[curIdx + 1] : null;

  const tabs = [
    { id: 'classroom', label: '1. Concept Classroom (Phase 2)' },
    { id: 'decoder', label: '2. Question Decoder (Phase 3)' },
    { id: 'viz', label: '3. Visualization Lab (Phase 4)' },
    { id: 'solution', label: '4. Guided & Compare Solutions (Phase 5 & 5A)' },
    { id: 'exam', label: '5. Exam Answer (Phase 6)' },
    { id: 'memory', label: '6. Memory Palace (Phase 7)' },
    { id: 'practice', label: '7. Practice Arena (Phase 8)' }
  ];

  return `
    <div class="q-selector-bar">
      <span style="font-size:0.8rem; font-weight:700; color:var(--text-muted); margin-right:0.3rem;">Select Question:</span>
      ${ordered.map(item => `
        <button class="q-pill ${item.id === q.id ? 'active' : ''}" onclick="selectQuestion('${item.id}')">
          <span>${ escapeHtml(item.shortTitle) }</span>
          <span>${ state.mastery[item.id] === 'mastered' ? '✓' : state.mastery[item.id] === 'needs_revision' ? '⚠️' : '' }</span>
        </button>
      `).join('')}
    </div>

    <div class="card" style="padding:1rem 1.25rem; margin-bottom:0.9rem;">
      <div style="display:flex; flex-wrap:wrap; justify-content:space-between; align-items:center; gap:0.75rem;">
        <div>
          <span class="badge badge-blue">Original #${q.number} • Prerequisite Step ${q.pedagogicalOrder}</span>
          <span class="badge badge-amber">⏱️ ${q.estTimeMins} mins • ${escapeHtml(q.examLength)}</span>
          <h2 style="font-size:1.25rem; margin-top:0.3rem;">${escapeHtml(q.title)}</h2>
        </div>
        <div style="display:flex; flex-wrap:wrap; gap:0.45rem; align-items:center;">
          <button class="btn" onclick="toggleBookmark('${q.id}')">${state.bookmarks[q.id] ? '⭐ Bookmarked' : '☆ Bookmark'}</button>
          <select class="util-select" onchange="setMastery('${q.id}', this.value)" aria-label="Question Mastery Status">
            <option value="not_started" ${state.mastery[q.id]==='not_started'?'selected':''}>Status: Not Started</option>
