The real dataset and E0 rescoring have been executed. Notebook no-inference paths have been checked; see `artifacts/notebook_validation.json` and `artifacts/verification.json` for recorded checks. E1 predictions are partially saved; E1–E4 are incomplete. Consult timestamped live status files for current counts. The initial no-inference delivery had no configured connection. The controller extension now uses the user-confirmed shell-configured Azure deployment; consult its live status files for actual progress. Missing results are never filled with simulated values. Existing historical logs are rescored but their original backend execution cannot be independently authenticated from those logs alone.

## Setup and real runs

From the project directory:

```bash
python -m pip install -r WEBExtension/requirements.txt
python -m jupyter lab WEBExtension
```

Use a dedicated environment and save the actual resolved package versions with each run. `artifacts/environment_versions.json` records the environment used for local verification; dependency ranges above are compatibility constraints, not a claim that every supported version is identical.

Edit `config.json`:

- Select `provider="azure"` or `"openai_compatible"` (a real hosted model or local OpenAI-compatible model server, including vLLM). Mock providers are rejected.
- Set `model` and `model_revision` to the exact deployed model/deployment identity. Mutable aliases cannot guarantee rerun equivalence; save returned provider model identifiers and responses.
- Set the corresponding **matching** Hugging Face `tokenizer` and an immutable `tokenizer_revision` commit. No word counting or token-estimate fallback is allowed for payloads.
- Set a positive `max_calls`. SDK retries are disabled and failed calls count against the cap. A cap is per frozen run directory; replication multiplies the total.
- Use environment `AZURE_OPENAI_API_KEY`, `AZURE_OPENAI_ENDPOINT` for Azure, or `OPENAI_API_KEY` and `base_url` for a real compatible endpoint. Never write keys into config or notebook output. `api_version` is configurable and must be supported by your deployment.
- Confirm that `max_tokens`/temperature are supported by the backend before scaling. Unsupported parameters fail explicitly. No hidden adaptation or model substitution occurs.
- Confirm model context size with `context_limit`. A conservative local prompt estimate checks overflow; provider input usage is the measured count when available.
- Keep sample/partition/role/crop seeds and payload budget fixed. Changing the protocol creates another run directory.

Set `EXECUTE=True` in the desired notebook's setup cell to run it. The default is no inference. Credentials are not needed for E0, data checks or previews. E1 includes a SelfUpdate control by default: 24 calls/question, 7,200 for 300. Without it: 18/question, but CW−NoComm combines additional inference with communication. E2 standalone: 9/question including initials, or zero additional physical calls after identical E1 R. E3 adds 2/question after shared initials. E4 standalone: 3 initial + 3×depth/question. Default E1–E4 full suite: at most 11,400 calls before cache reuse; this is not a price estimate.

Run a separate 10–20 question invariant pilot first. Inspect malformed responses, source-citation validity, exact slices, cached identities, measured input/output distributions, and evidence availability before a primary run. Do not choose interventions by which pilot answers look better. Use the same fixed primary questions for every treatment. A per-run file lock blocks concurrent inference notebooks from duplicating requests. All failures stop and are logged; no fabricated answer repairs. Raw complete responses are cached immediately, before parsing, so malformed responses remain inspectable without extra billed calls. In-flight requests and ambiguous transport/server failures must be reconciled with provider logs before retrying. Each attempt has a unique ID and a saved pre-call request. `backend.reconcile_no_generation` is a manual attestation tool requiring saved provider evidence that no completion was generated; it must never be called on the basis of a timeout alone. A response saved before a crash is recovered automatically from its original attempt.

## Statistical and scientific limits

This is an instrumented controlled evaluation, not a claim of new state-of-the-art routing. Candidate-context BM25 is a reproducible baseline, not a claim that it is universally the strongest retriever. The blueprint's new intervention is identifiable only under its stated controls. The primary E1 interaction is frozen; CCW, within-regime, self-update, content and stopping contrasts are exploratory. Holm correction applies within an experiment; do not interpret across-experiment explorations as a single confirmatory family. Supporting-label overlap is not citation entailment. Gold supports never enter inference-time selection.

Payloads preserve real quoted source sentences after validating model-selected citations. Generated peer answers remain explicitly unverified. Payload quotas can crop source lines; only complete quoted lines count as delivered facts. Prefix/crop identity and short-message cases are retained, not filtered to exaggerate effects. All assigned evidence availability failures stay in primary analysis. The `support_split` subgroup is post-hoc, not a gold-selected sampling criterion.

Offline stopping replay reports hypothetical avoided calls. A quality-preservation claim requires a positive, task-justified F1 margin frozen before execution and a confidence lower bound above negative margin. Missing provider usage remains unknown. Separate logical condition costs (including shared initials) from once-per-request physical usage; errors may have unknown billable usage. No dollar savings or measured speedups are inferred.

Three seed replications use the same questions; uncertainty clusters by question after seed averaging and must not count 600 independent questions from 200×3 runs. Report individual seeds and independent backbones, with negative/null results. The supplied blueprint's optional second benchmark is not implemented or claimed as evaluated; independent-backbone replication is supported on the authentic HotpotQA sample. A new benchmark needs a documented loader, scoring protocol and separate preregistration rather than relabeling HotpotQA.

## Files and reproducibility

- `data/`: pinned publisher-hosted parquet, lossless JSON export, download/source hashes and tokenizer cache.
- `artifacts/legacy_audit/`: real saved legacy predictions rescored; CSV tables and audit checks.
- `artifacts/run_<hash>/`: frozen config/source/data hashes, source snapshots and resolved package versions, ordered sample, gold-free input whitelist, exact requests/responses, call ledger, complete question checkpoints, reports.
- `protocol.py`, `backend.py`, `experiments.py`, `reporting.py`, `robustness.py`, `audit.py`: shared notebook implementations. These do not import project mock backends.
- `tests/`: software invariants only, never research observations. Test-only codecs/fixtures cannot enter the experiment runner.
- `references/`: supplied blueprint/strategy and unmodified upstream HotpotQA scoring script.

The E0 audit finds equal saved proxy volume for corrected W4 Static_Role/Learned_SparseComm/Random_Routing on all 100 questions. `Complete_Broadcast_Matched` differs from Static_Role on 45 questions. These are word proxies, not measured model tokens. Current-source checksums cannot establish what code historically executed. Some legacy answers are empty; no repaired answer is substituted.

Official sources: [HotpotQA](https://hotpotqa.github.io/), [publisher dataset](https://huggingface.co/datasets/hotpotqa/hotpot_qa), [upstream scorer](https://github.com/hotpotqa/hotpot/blob/master/hotpot_evaluate_v1.py), [tokenizer documentation](https://huggingface.co/docs/transformers/main_classes/tokenizer). HotpotQA data are CC BY-SA 4.0; retain attribution to Yang et al., EMNLP 2018. The publisher revision and file SHA-256s are recorded; the original CMU host timed out and the pinned publisher mirror was used.

```bash
python -m unittest discover -s WEBExtension/tests -v
python WEBExtension/validate_notebooks.py
```

Validation executes all twelve notebooks with inference disabled. It cannot substitute for a real backend pilot or a completed research run.

## Controller retraining extension — 14 October 2026

Start with `09_Controller_Splits_Leakage_Pilot.ipynb`, then `10_Controller_Train_Validate_Select.ipynb` and `11_Controller_Heldout_Comparison.ipynb`. The thirteen notebooks include the original controlled E0–E4 evaluation plus a new learned-policy study E5. The default primary evaluation now uses 300 frozen held-out questions shared with the controller study.

`controller_config.json` now declares 100 optimization, 30 validation, 300 held-out and 12 pilot questions, three optimizer seeds and three collection epochs. All training data come from the publisher's pinned authentic HotpotQA train parquet. Pairwise question IDs, normalized questions and candidate-document titles are disjoint. The 100 known legacy Web evaluation IDs are excluded from the held-out set. Source hashes, exact partition IDs, type counts, zero-overlap checks and limitations are in the frozen split manifest. Title filtering changes representativeness; it does not establish absence of all semantic duplicates or model pretraining contamination. Training contexts and development fullwiki candidates also have different retrieval distributions.

The new small CPU controller preserves SparseComm's task/communication objective but uses **categorical contextual-bandit PPO**, with graph/quota/NoComm actions sampled and differentiated by PyTorch. This is not an architecture-compatible warm start from old continuous-action weights or a claim that multi-round stopping was trained. The old checkpoints are preserved. No correctness history, gold answer, supporting-label score or ID hash enters its observable features. Receiver calls and provider usage count only recipients actually updated.

Validation alone selects checkpoints and one of three named static candidate graphs. A checkpoint/hash gate closes training before held-out scoring. Held-out comparison includes NoComm, self-update, selected static, broadcast and random graphs matched on edge count/quota. Report seeds individually and average within each question for uncertainty. A fixed action or learned NoComm decision must be reported, rather than tuned away after inspecting test results. No statistical procedure or sample size guarantees reviewer approval or positive findings.

The user's existing Azure gpt-oss-120b deployment is configured via the Azure v1 base URL. `machine_connection.py` reads literal Azure exports from `~/.bashrc` in memory, preferring environment settings, and never writes credentials. The older Python-backend credential returned 401; the current shell-configured endpoint is accessible. Tokenizer revision is pinned to the official publisher commit. The deployment alias's immutable backend snapshot is not independently exposed: raw returned model identifiers and exact responses remain the reproduction record.

Run from the project directory:

```bash
python WEBExtension/run_controller_machine.py forecast
python WEBExtension/run_controller_machine.py pilot
python WEBExtension/run_controller_machine.py train
python WEBExtension/run_controller_machine.py evaluate
```

`all` runs pilot → train → evaluate in order. Notebook execution remains disabled by default; the machine CLI explicitly runs real inference. The three-seed training/evaluation suite has a conservative upper bound of 26,340 calls before exact prompt reuse (the preserved original 300/100 study forecast was 43,800), with an explicit operational cap of 46,000; pilot calls are separately logged. The independent E1–E4 suite has a cap of 11,400 calls for 300 questions. These are completion caps, not prices. Same-prompt initial and receiver responses are reused with model/decoding/tokenizer identity checks. No dummy rollouts, simulated rewards or rewritten answers enter research outputs.

Actual progress is recorded in the active `controller_study_*/pilot_status.json`, `training_status.json`, per-seed `selection.json`, `E5_status.json` and `machine_run_status.json`. A study is trained only after authentic complete training rollouts and validation selection; it is evaluated only after a complete held-out artifact passes the reporting gate. Do not infer success from software tests or empty preview manifests.

### Gold-location stratification

[12_Gold_Location_Stratified_Analysis.ipynb](12_Gold_Location_Stratified_Analysis.ipynb) reports Disjoint-Gold and Co-located-Gold communication effects, plus Retrieval-Missing-Gold and Undefined-Gold groups. It reconstructs actual frozen BM25 top-six retrieval and exclusive P sharding; no gold-aware resharding occurs. The pooled primary estimand is retained. Question-paired bootstrap intervals and an exploratory difference in effects are reported without assuming a favorable result. See [analysis/STRATIFICATION_PLAN.md](analysis/STRATIFICATION_PLAN.md).

Real machine jobs were launched after the 12-question invariant pilot completed: 176 successful Azure gpt-oss-120b calls, no provider errors, all normal stop finishes. Two invalid citation references were observed and retained as diagnostics. This is feasibility evidence, not a trained-policy quality result. Training and held-out comparisons are incomplete until their status artifacts say COMPLETE. The controlled E1–E4 pipeline is `python WEBExtension/analysis/run_controlled_machine.py`; it generates pooled and stratified reports after each completed experiment.

Machine update: the first controller rollout stopped on an actual Azure output content-filter refusal (HTTP 400), whose diagnostic reports 1,817 prompt tokens and 154 completion tokens. No answer was substituted, no filter bypass attempted, and no checkpoint selection or held-out learned-policy claim is complete. Provider-refusal handling is awaiting the user's protocol preference.

The first E1 attempt exposed an incorrect invariant comparison: CW and CCW list edges in different orders. The check now compares source text/token identity by sender. The corrected controlled run inherits 38 verified exact-response cache entries and their original ledger; `inherited_inference.json` records parent hashes. These inherited attempts are the same calls, so parent/child ledger totals must never be added. No generated benchmark examples were introduced.

### User-approved provider refusal policy and completion cap

The user approved `provider_refusal_policy=abstain_with_reported_usage`. Explicit provider `content_filter` or structured refusal markers yield an empty scored answer and no citations, with `provider_refusal=true`; this is a recorded abstention, never an invented model answer. Exact HTTP error/completion bodies and reported usage remain in authenticated response files. Input filters without usage remain unknown, and other errors or malformed/truncated outputs still halt. Reports show refusal counts and include reported refused-call costs.

Every arm now has an 8,192-token output cap because a real 2,048-token request exhausted its budget before returning answer text. This operational cap revision is applied uniformly before complete held-out results were inspected. Earlier different-cap requests remain archived and are not primary responses for the new frozen run. No provider filter is bypassed. The current source/configuration hashes determine new study directories. `python WEBExtension/run_controller_machine.py all` completes pilot, training, frozen evaluation, and pooled/stratified E5 reports when every phase succeeds.

### Shared Azure request pacing

The 8,192-token requests encountered Azure HTTP429 rate-limit rejections. Active pipelines now run through `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1 python -u WEBExtension/analysis/rate_limited_machine.py controller` and the corresponding `controlled` argument. These share one provider/model request lock and cooldown, initially four seconds between completed requests. Actual Retry-After guidance controls cooldown; repeated429 events increase pacing. Only HTTP429 rejections without a completion body are retried, at most four times per completion; every attempt remains in the original ledger and counts against the operational call cap. Filter refusals, malformed outputs, truncations, timeouts and other errors are not retried.

The pacing script does not change scientific request parameters, datasets, selected actions or caches. Its hash and frozen copy are recorded separately in each active run's `operational_rate_policy.json` and `operational_source_snapshot`. Reported response latency now includes client queue/pacing time; it must not be described as pure provider generation latency. Physical usage for failed attempts remains unknown when the provider did not report it. Live paths are in `artifacts/active_machine_runs.json`; incomplete runs never become result tables.

### Durable continuation after the interrupted chat sessions

All 1,746 previously saved current-cap provider responses were checksum-validated. The two missing responses could not be recovered: the stored-completion endpoint returned no records, and no matching local response/temp file existed. The user authorized only those replacement attempts; their original outcome/cost liabilities remain unknown and potentially billed. Frozen prompts, data, PPO math and checkpoint-selection rules are unchanged.

The running pipelines are now independent systemd user services. See `artifacts/active_machine_runs.json` for unit names and log paths and each study's `runtime_progress.json` for a timestamped live heartbeat. Worker stdout/stderr is saved in `service_logs`. All raw response bodies, errors, request receipts, parsed outputs, timings, actual PPO batches, per-step weights/optimizer/gradients/RNG, and versioned collection/validation checkpoints are stored locally. Dataset parquet sources are also preserved in `data/controller_training_sources`. Details: [PERSISTENCE_AND_RECOVERY.md](PERSISTENCE_AND_RECOVERY.md).

The machine must remain powered on; closing the chat does not stop these service-managed jobs. Workers never restart blindly after a new ambiguous request. Do not launch duplicate notebook inference while the services own the run locks. Notebook analysis cells can read completed results without issuing new calls.

## Active 100/30/300 sample-size revision

The user requested 100 training questions per collection epoch, 30 validation questions per epoch, and 300 separate test questions. Three collection epochs and seeds 7/19/31 remain. The same fixed questions are reused each epoch: 200 training contexts and 60 validation contexts per epoch across R/P, yielding 1,800 training rollouts across all seeds/epochs. Outcome-independent type-stratified subsets of the original train/validation partitions retain 80/20 and 24/6 bridge/comparison questions. Held-out gold/input files are byte-identical to the original 300-question test files. All pairwise question/document-title overlap checks remain zero.

The original controller study is stopped and preserved. The new study starts fresh controllers; no old rollouts or optimizer weights are relabeled as new training. Authentic completed provider responses are copied byte-for-byte with checksums and original source ledger references to avoid paying again for identical prompts. New physical-call accounting excludes those already-paid calls; `inference/cache_import_manifest.json` preserves their original usage and provenance for combined accounting without double-counting. Original missing/unknown-cost attempts remain in the original ledgers. The revision record is `artifacts/sample_size_revision_100_30_300/revision.json`; active paths are in `artifacts/active_machine_runs.json`. E1–E4 retain their 300-question protocol and are still stopped after the previously reported timeout.

The active services now use adaptive start pacing and at most two concurrent network requests across the two independent pipelines. See `PERSISTENCE_AND_RECOVERY.md`; request/checkpoint math is unchanged. Fixed scientific samples are 100 training, 30 validation and 300 separate held-out questions. Laptop power/internet and awake state are required.
